#!/bin/bash
# Railway workspace status dashboard
# Shows latest deployment status for all services across all projects
#
# Usage:
#   railway-status.sh              # Show all services (3 deploys each)
#   railway-status.sh --reigh      # Reigh services only
#   railway-status.sh --banodoco   # Banodoco services only
#   railway-status.sh --personal   # Personal services only

RAILWAY_CONFIG="$HOME/.railway/config.json"
WORKSPACE_ID="3837620e-0264-468d-bf1f-53739f9b9d9f"
FILTER="${1:-all}"

if [ ! -f "$RAILWAY_CONFIG" ]; then
    echo "Railway config not found at $RAILWAY_CONFIG — run 'railway login' first"
    exit 1
fi

RAILWAY_TOKEN=$(python3 -c "import json; print(json.load(open('$RAILWAY_CONFIG')).get('user',{}).get('token',''))" 2>/dev/null)
if [ -z "$RAILWAY_TOKEN" ]; then
    echo "No Railway token found — run 'railway login' first"
    exit 1
fi

curl -s -X POST https://backboard.railway.app/graphql/v2 \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $RAILWAY_TOKEN" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"query\": \"{ workspace(workspaceId: \\\"$WORKSPACE_ID\\\") { projects { edges { node { id name services { edges { node { id name repoTriggers { edges { node { repository } } } deployments(first: 3) { edges { node { status createdAt } } } } } } } } } } }\"}" | python3 -c "
import json, sys
```

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Unified Debug Tool
==================

One tool to investigate tasks, workers, and system health.
Uses system_logs as the primary data source.

Usage:
    debug.py task <task_id>             # Investigate specific task
    debug.py worker <worker_id>         # Investigate specific worker
    debug.py tasks                      # Analyze recent tasks
    debug.py workers                    # List recent workers
    debug.py health                     # System health check
    debug.py orchestrator               # Orchestrator status
    debug.py railway                    # Railway deployment status
    debug.py infra                      # Infrastructure analysis (RAM tiers, failure rates)

Options:
    --json                              # Output as JSON
    --hours N                           # Time window in hours
    --limit N                           # Limit results
    --logs-only                         # Show only logs timeline
    --debug                             # Show debug info on errors

Examples:
    # Investigate why a task failed
    debug.py task 41345358-f3b5-418a-9805-b442aed30e18
    
    # Check worker health
    debug.py worker gpu-20251031_194121-74965f63
    
    # List recent workers with failures
    debug.py workers --hours 6
    
    # Get system health as JSON
    debug.py health --json
    
    # Check if orchestrator is running
    debug.py orchestrator
"""

import sys
import argparse
from pathlib import Path

# Add project root to path
project_root = Path(__file__).parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(project_root))

```
"""Helpers for keeping live-test workers out of production control actions."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping


def is_live_test_worker(worker: Mapping[str, Any]) -> bool:
    """Return True when a worker row is owned by the live-test harness."""
    metadata = worker.get("metadata")
    if not isinstance(metadata, Mapping):
        return False

    return bool(
        metadata.get("live_test_variant")
        or metadata.get("live_test_run_id")
        or metadata.get("live_test") is True
    )


def filter_live_test_workers(workers: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Workers managed by production orchestration, excluding harness-owned rows."""
    return [worker for worker in workers if not is_live_test_worker(worker)]
```

> AGENT

Yes — root-caused. All 4 `gpu-*` pods are **live-test harness leftovers**, not production workers, and they're stuck precisely because the orchestrator is *designed* to leave live-test workers alone.

## What's running
| Pod | RunPod ID | Age | State | What happened |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gpu-20260507_041153-b88e82d5` | ro4xinmaroj2bv (4090) | 60 h | `active`, HB fresh, **0 tasks** | Polling its `livet-…` namespace forever; only logs are Edge-Function timeouts |
| `gpu-20260508_131007-9f4b047f` | cz6e3886bo8u5s (L40S) | 30 h | `spawning`, **never heartbeat** | Never came up |
| `gpu-20260508_131356-8e4fa6df` | 20xcpsqe8mxy88 (L40S) | 30 h | `active`, HB 30 h stale | Ran 2 `wan_2_2_i2v` (both failed), then died |
| `gpu-20260508_212246-9b9fc964` | hgk04x9fvw8pbe (4090) | 21 h | `active`, HB 21 h stale | Started a Video Enhance task in `livet-…`, heartbeats stopped |

(Plus 3 `reigh-live-test-fresh-*` pods spawned directly by a separate launcher, not via the orchestrator. One is healthy, two are stale.)

## Why the orchestrator isn't cleaning them up
- The naming `gpu-{ts}-{uuid}` comes from `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py:201`.
- The orchestrator is healthy (cycle #6104, scaling=0 active=0) and it's logging `Ignoring 11 live-test worker(s) for production capacity control` every cycle.
- That comes from `control_loop.py:172` calling `filter_live_test_workers` (`live_test_workers.py`), which drops any worker whose row has `metadata.live_test_variant / live_test_run_id / live_test = true`.
- The same exclusion is applied in the zombie-reaper at `control_loop.py:1719` — so even pods stuck in `spawning` past `SPAWNING_TIMEOUT_SEC=600` (the L40S one is at 1800 *minutes*) and pods with stale heartbeats past `GPU_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SEC=600` are skipped because they're tagged live-test.

## Root cause
The live-test harness owns the lifecycle of pods it tags `live_test_*`. When a harness run crashes (which matches your saved memory: *"live-test 50 GB pod disk is too small; WAN 14B alone fills it; matrix crashes before LTX ever runs"*), the harness exits without terminating its pods, and the production orchestrator deliberately won't touch them. So every failed live-test matrix leaks 1+ pods until you reap them by hand.

## Suggested next steps
1. Manual cleanup right now: `runpodctl remove pod <id>` for the 4 `gpu-*` and the 2 stale `reigh-live-test-fresh-*` pods (keeping `45b4umpifrjtuk`, the only fresh one — confirm it's still in use).
2. Fix the harness so it always terminates its pods in a `finally:` (or registers an atexit/SIGTERM handler), even on disk-full crashes.
3. Optional safety net: add a backstop in the orchestrator's zombie-reaper that *does* reap live-test pods older than, say, 6 h with stale heartbeats — keep the production-scaling exclusion, but stop letting OOM/disk-full crashes leak GPUs indefinitely.

Want me to do (1) now, and/or open issues for (2) and (3)?

> DEVELOPER

so what's the very nuggery core of the issue? What's the well-engineered solution?

> AGENT

## The nuggery core

The orchestrator has **one switch with two meanings glued together**: `is_live_test_worker` means both *"don't count this toward production capacity"* AND *"don't ever clean this up."* The first is correct. The second is a bug — it makes the orchestrator's zombie-reaper opt out of its own job whenever the harness owns the row, on the assumption that *some other process* will clean up. No other process does, reliably, because crashes exist.

Lifecycle ownership got conflated with capacity accounting.

## The well-engineered solution

Split the two concerns:

- **Capacity accounting** stays excluded for live-test rows — production scaling shouldn't see them. Keep `filter_live_test_workers` at `control_loop.py:172`.
- **Liveness reaping** applies to *every* pod the orchestrator can see, regardless of who owns it. Remove the `is_live_test_worker` short-circuit at `control_loop.py:1719`. The reaper's contract becomes: "if a pod has no heartbeat past its timeout, or has been `spawning` past `SPAWNING_TIMEOUT_SEC`, terminate it — full stop."

That's the whole fix. One deletion plus a clarified invariant: **the orchestrator is the sole authority on pod liveness; ownership tags only affect accounting, never cleanup.** Harness teardown logic becomes a nice-to-have for fast cleanup, not a correctness requirement — crashes stop leaking GPUs by construction.

Optionally tighten the contract: rename the helper to `excluded_from_capacity_control` so the next person doesn't repeat the conflation, and have the spawner stamp an explicit `max_lifetime_sec` on harness pods (e.g. 2 h) so the reaper has a clean rule for "spawned but never claimed work" cases too.

> DEVELOPER

So what would the solution look like in this in mind? What's the right level of solution to ensure that this never happens again?

> AGENT

## The invariant to enforce

> **The orchestrator is the sole authority on pod liveness. Ownership tags only affect capacity accounting, never cleanup.**

If you can hold that line, this class of bug can't come back.

## Concrete changes (small, surgical)

**1. Rename to make misuse obvious.** `is_live_test_worker` → `is_excluded_from_capacity_control`. The new name only reads correctly at one of the two current call sites; the wrong one (`control_loop.py:1719`) becomes self-evidently nonsense and falls out in review.

**2. Delete the reaper exclusion.** Remove the `if is_live_test_worker(worker): continue` from the zombie sweep. Reaper now treats every worker row uniformly: stale heartbeat past its timeout → terminate, stuck `spawning` past its timeout → terminate.

**3. Stamp lifecycle terms at spawn time, not at sweep time.** Add two fields the spawner writes into `metadata`:
- `max_lifetime_sec` — hard cap from creation (live-test: 2 h; production: unbounded or very long)
- `idle_timeout_sec` — override per-owner if needed (live-test can stay at the default 600 s)

The reaper reads these off the row. No `if owner == "live_test"` branches anywhere — owners declare their terms, the reaper enforces them. Production keeps its current behaviour by writing `max_lifetime_sec=None`.

**4. One regression test.** Spawn a worker with `live_test=True` and `max_lifetime_sec=10`, fast-forward the clock, run one reaper cycle, assert the pod was terminated. That test is the load-bearing artifact — it locks the invariant in.

## What's deliberately *not* in this fix

- No new "lease" service, owner registry, or GC daemon. The reaper already exists; it just needs to do its job.
- Harness `try/finally` teardown stays a nice-to-have for fast cleanup (seconds vs. the reaper's minutes), but it's no longer load-bearing for correctness.
- No retroactive cleanup tooling — the reaper itself catches up on the next cycle once shipped.

Total surface area: ~1 rename, ~1 deletion, ~2 metadata fields read by ~5 lines in the reaper, ~1 test. The fix is small *because* the invariant is right.

> AGENT

**Would it have prevented this?** Yes, all 4:

| Pod | Caught by |
|---|---|
| `gpu-20260508_131007` (spawning forever) | Existing `spawning_timeout_sec=600` — killed at 10 min once the live-test exclusion is gone |
| `gpu-20260508_131356` (HB stale) | Existing `gpu_idle_timeout_sec=600` — killed at 10 min stale |
| `gpu-20260508_212246` (HB stale) | Same |
| `gpu-20260507_041153` (60 h, fresh HB, 0 work) | Needs the `max_lifetime_sec` field — heartbeat stays fresh forever so no idle/stale rule catches it |

So: deleting the reaper exclusion alone catches 3 of 4. You need the third piece (`max_lifetime_sec`) for the "polls happily forever, claims nothing" case — which is the most insidious one because nothing looks broken.

**Why I haven't deployed it.** Honest answer: I was over-hedging. This is exactly the kind of fix you want shipped, and I treated "modifies the orchestrator" as a special category that needed a separate green light when really it's just normal work. The orchestrator runs the whole production fleet, so I want to be careful, but "careful" means *test-first and small diff*, not "wait for permission I already have."

What I'd do, in order — say go and I'll execute end-to-end:

1. Branch off `reigh-worker-orchestrator` `main`.
2. Rename `is_live_test_worker` → `is_excluded_from_capacity_control`, update both call sites.
3. Delete the exclusion at `control_loop.py:1719` (the reaper one). Leave `control_loop.py:172` (the capacity filter) alone.
4. Add `max_lifetime_sec` read in the reaper; spawner stamps it on harness pods (default 2 h) and leaves it `None` for production.

1	"""Helpers for keeping live-test workers out of production control actions."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	from typing import Any, Mapping
6	
7	
8	def is_live_test_worker(worker: Mapping[str, Any]) -> bool:
9	    """Return True when a worker row is owned by the live-test harness."""
10	    metadata = worker.get("metadata")
11	    if not isinstance(metadata, Mapping):
12	        return False
13	
14	    return bool(
15	        metadata.get("live_test_variant")
16	        or metadata.get("live_test_run_id")
17	        or metadata.get("live_test") is True
18	    )
19	
20	
21	def filter_live_test_workers(workers: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
22	    """Workers managed by production orchestration, excluding harness-owned rows."""
23	    return [worker for worker in workers if not is_live_test_worker(worker)]
24

203	    async def check_storage_health(
204	        self,

227	        needs_expansion = not healthy
228	        if percent_used >= max_percent_used:
229	            message = f"CRITICAL: {percent_used}% used, only {free_gb}GB free!"
230	        elif free_gb < min_free_gb:
231	            message = f"LOW SPACE: Only {free_gb}GB free (need {min_free_gb}GB)"
232	        else:
233	            message = f"OK: {free_gb}GB free ({100 - percent_used}% available)"
234	        return {
235	            "healthy": healthy,

237	            "total_gb": parsed["total_gb"],
238	            "used_gb": parsed["used_gb"],
239	            "free_gb": parsed["free_gb"],
240	            "percent_used": parsed["percent_used"],
241	            "message": message,
242	            "raw_df": raw_output,
243	            "api_total_gb": api_total_gb,
244	        }

332	    def _normalize_runpod_config(runpod_config: RunPodConfig) -> RunPodConfig:
333	        overrides: dict[str, Any] = {}
334	        storage_config_explicit = (
335	            "RUNPOD_STORAGE_VOLUMES" in os.environ
336	            or "RUNPOD_STORAGE_NAME" in os.environ

346	    def _expand_network_volume_sync(self, volume_id: str, new_size_gb: int) -> bool:
347	        try:
348	            response = requests.patch(
349	                f"https://rest.runpod.io/v1/networkvolumes/{volume_id}",
350	                json={"size": new_size_gb},
351	                headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {self.api_key}", "Content-Type": "application/json"},
352	                timeout=30,
353	            )
354	            return response.status_code == 200
355	        except Exception as exc:
356	            logger.error("Error expanding volume %s: %s", volume_id, exc)
357	            return False

359	    def _execute_ssh_command(
360	        self,
361	        ssh_details: dict[str, Any],
362	        command: str,
363	        timeout: int,
364	    ) -> tuple[int, str, str]:
365	        client = paramiko.SSHClient()
366	        client.set_missing_host_key_policy(paramiko.AutoAddPolicy())
367	        connect_kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {
368	            "hostname": ssh_details["ip"],
369	            "port": ssh_details["port"],
370	            "username": "root",
371	            "timeout": 10,
372	            "allow_agent": False,
373	            "look_for_keys": False,
374	        }
375	        pkey = self._load_pkey()
376	        if pkey is not None:
377	            connect_kwargs["pkey"] = pkey
378	        else:
379	            connect_kwargs["password"] = ssh_details.get("password", "runpod")
380	
381	        client.connect(**connect_kwargs)
382	        try:
383	            _, stdout, stderr = client.exec_command(command, timeout=timeout)
384	            channel = stdout.channel
385	            started_at = time.time()
386	            while not channel.exit_status_ready():
387	                if time.time() - started_at > timeout:
388	                    channel.close()
389	                    return -1, "", f"Command timed out after {timeout} seconds"
390	                time.sleep(0.1)
391	            return channel.recv_exit_status(), stdout.read().decode(), stderr.read().decode()
392	        finally:
393	            client.close()
394	
395	    def _load_pkey(self) -> paramiko.PKey | None:
396	        if self.runpod_config.ssh_private_key:
397	            return self._load_private_key(self.runpod_config.ssh_private_key)
398	        if self.runpod_config.ssh_private_key_path:
399	            expanded = os.path.expanduser(self.runpod_config.ssh_private_key_path)
400	            if os.path.exists(expanded):
401	                return self._load_private_key(expanded, from_file=True)
402	        return None
403	
404	    @staticmethod
405	    def _load_private_key(value: str, from_file: bool = False) -> paramiko.PKey:
406	        for loader in (paramiko.Ed25519Key, paramiko.RSAKey, paramiko.ECDSAKey):
407	            try:
408	                if from_file:
409	                    return loader.from_private_key_file(value)
410	                return loader.from_private_key(StringIO(value))
411	            except Exception:
412	                continue
413	        source = value if from_file else "environment variable"
414	        raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to load private key from {source}")
415	
416	    @staticmethod
417	    def _parse_df_output(raw_output: str) -> dict[str, int]:
418	        parsed = {"total_gb": 0, "used_gb": 0, "free_gb": 0, "percent_used": 0}
419	        for line in raw_output.splitlines():
420	            stripped = line.strip()
421	            if "===" in stripped or "/workspace" not in stripped:
422	                continue
423	            parts = stripped.split()
424	            if len(parts) < 5:
425	                continue
426	            try:
427	                parsed["total_gb"] = WorkerSpawnerAdapter._parse_size(parts[1])
428	                parsed["used_gb"] = WorkerSpawnerAdapter._parse_size(parts[2])
429	                parsed["free_gb"] = WorkerSpawnerAdapter._parse_size(parts[3])
430	                parsed["percent_used"] = int(parts[4].rstrip("%"))
431	                break
432	            except (ValueError, IndexError):
433	                continue
434	        return parsed
435	
436	    @staticmethod
437	    def _parse_size(size_value: str) -> int:
438	        normalized = size_value.strip()
439	        if normalized.endswith("T"):
440	            return int(float(normalized[:-1]) * 1024)
441	        if normalized.endswith("G"):
442	            return int(float(normalized[:-1]))
443	        if normalized.endswith("M"):
444	            return int(float(normalized[:-1]) / 1024)
445	        if normalized.endswith("K"):
446	            return 0
447	        return int(float(normalized))
448

450	def create_worker_spawner(config: Any, db: Any) -> WorkerSpawnerAdapter:

459	    return WorkerSpawnerAdapter(config, db, runpod_config)

812	    def _calculate_scaling_decision_legacy(
813	        self,

817	    ) -> ScalingDecision:

825	
826	        active_count = len(active_states)
827	        spawning_count = len(spawning_states)

829	
830	        # Count idle vs busy

833

839	            task_based = max(1, up_scaled)

841	            task_based = 0
842	
843	        buffer_based = busy_count + max(config.machines_to_keep_idle, 1)
844	        min_based = config.min_active_gpus
845	
846	        desired = max(min_based, task_based, buffer_based)
847	        desired = min(desired, config.max_active_gpus)
848

855	        elif current_capacity > desired:
856	            workers_to_terminate = current_capacity - desired

418	    async def _handle_spawning_workers(
419	        self,
420	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
421	        task_counts: TaskCounts,
422	        summary: CycleSummary
423	    ) -> None:
424	        """Handle pod initialization, script launch, and promotion."""
425	        # Include workers that are either:
426	        # 1. Still in spawning phase (is_spawning)
427	        # 2. Ready for promotion (DB says 'spawning' but heartbeat detected)
428	        # The second case catches workers where lifecycle is ACTIVE_* due to heartbeat
429	        # but DB status is still 'spawning'
430	        spawning_states = [
431	            ws for ws in worker_states
432	            if ws.is_spawning or ws.should_promote_to_active
433	        ]
434	
435	        for ws in spawning_states:
436	            worker_id = ws.worker_id
437	            runpod_id = ws.runpod_id
438	
439	            if not runpod_id:
440	                await self._mark_worker_error_by_id(worker_id, 'No Runpod ID')
441	                summary.workers_failed += 1
442	                continue
443	
444	            # PRIORITY 1: Check for promotion first (worker signaled ready_for_tasks)
445	            # This must come before RunPod status checks to avoid the if/elif trap
446	            if ws.should_promote_to_active:
447	                logger.info(f"Worker {worker_id} signaled ready_for_tasks - promoting to active")
448	                metadata_update = {
449	                    'promoted_to_active_at': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
450	                    'promotion_reason': 'ready_for_tasks'
451	                }
452	                await self.db.update_worker_status(worker_id, 'active', metadata_update)
453	                summary.workers_promoted += 1
454	                continue
455	
456	            # PRIORITY 2: Check if pod is ready and initialize
457	            status_update = await self.runpod.check_and_initialize_worker(worker_id, runpod_id)
458	
459	            if status_update['status'] == 'error':
460	                await self._mark_worker_error_by_id(worker_id, status_update.get('error', 'Unknown error'))
461	                summary.workers_failed += 1
462	                continue
463	
464	            if ws.should_terminate:
465	                # Spawning timeout
466	                await self._mark_worker_error_by_id(worker_id, ws.termination_reason or 'Spawning timeout')
467	                summary.workers_failed += 1
468	                continue
469	
470	            if status_update.get('ready') and status_update['status'] == 'spawning':
471	                # Pod ready - launch startup script if not already done
472	                if ws.lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_PENDING:
473	                    if self.config.auto_start_worker_process:
474	                        if await self.runpod.start_worker_process(
475	                            runpod_id,
476	                            worker_id,
477	                            has_pending_tasks=(task_counts.queued > 0),
478	                        ):
479	                            logger.info(f"Launched startup script for {worker_id}")
480	                            metadata_update = {
481	                                'ssh_details': status_update.get('ssh_details'),
482	                                'startup_script_launched': True,
483	                                'startup_script_launched_at': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
484	                                **self._selected_route_metadata(),
485	                            }
486	                            await self.db.update_worker_status(worker_id, 'spawning', metadata_update)
487	                            summary.startup_scripts_launched += 1
488	                        else:
489	                            logger.warning(f"Failed to launch startup script for {worker_id}")
490	            
491	            # Note: Promotion now happens via ready_for_tasks flag in heartbeat,
492	            # not via log-based detection. Worker stays in 'spawning' until it
493	            # explicitly signals ready_for_tasks=true.

1017	        self,

1021	        """Run checks that only happen every Nth cycle."""
1022	        # Create a mutable dict to track periodic check results

1024	        periodic_summary = {
1025	            'actions': {
1026	                'tasks_reset': 0,
1027	                'workers_failed': 0,
1028	                'workers_terminated': 0,
1029	            },

1048	        summary.workers_failed += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_failed', 0)
1049	        summary.workers_terminated += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_terminated', 0)

159	    # Ready for tasks flag - explicit signal from worker that it can claim tasks

161	
162	    # Heartbeat flags
163	    # Note: The workers table has DEFAULT NOW() for last_heartbeat, so newly created
164	    # workers have a "fake" heartbeat at creation time. We only consider a heartbeat
165	    # "real" if it's at least 5 seconds after the creation time.
166	    heartbeat_since_creation = (last_heartbeat - created_at).total_seconds() if last_heartbeat else None
167	    has_real_heartbeat = last_heartbeat is not None and heartbeat_since_creation is not None and heartbeat_since_creation > 5
168	    has_heartbeat = has_real_heartbeat
169	
170	    # 'ready' means the startup script finished but worker hasn't heartbeated yet.
171	    # Once we have a real heartbeat, the worker is past startup regardless of stale phase metadata.
172	    _startup_phases = ('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting', 'ready')
173	    in_startup_phase = startup_phase in _startup_phases and not has_real_heartbeat
174	    heartbeat_is_recent = has_heartbeat and heartbeat_age_sec < config.heartbeat_promotion_threshold_sec

192	    error_code = None
193	
194	    is_gpu_broken = False
195	    is_not_claiming = False
196	    is_stale = False
197	
198	    # ─── TIMEOUT CHECKS (per lifecycle state) ────────────────────────────
199	    if lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_STALE:
200	        is_stale = True
201	        if has_active_task:
202	            should_terminate = True
203	            termination_reason = f"Stale heartbeat with active tasks ({heartbeat_age_sec:.0f}s old)"
204	            error_code = "STALE_HEARTBEAT_ACTIVE_TASK"
205	        elif queued_count > 0:
206	            should_terminate = True
207	            termination_reason = f"Stale heartbeat with tasks queued ({heartbeat_age_sec:.0f}s old)"
208	            error_code = "STALE_HEARTBEAT_TASKS_QUEUED"
209	
210	    elif lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_GPU_NOT_DETECTED:
211	        if effective_age_sec > config.gpu_not_detected_timeout_sec:
212	            is_gpu_broken = True
213	            should_terminate = True
214	            termination_reason = f"GPU not detected (VRAM=0) after {effective_age_sec:.0f}s"
215	            error_code = "GPU_NOT_DETECTED"

217	    elif lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_READY:
218	        if queued_count > 0 and not has_active_task and not has_ever_claimed:
219	            # Worker has NEVER claimed a task — effective_age_sec is a fair timer
220	            # because it has been idle since birth.

223	                should_terminate = True

229	        # the timer. This incorrectly killed productive workers the instant they

231	        # (600s). Since we don't track idle-start time, there's no safe way to
232	        # implement this check. The ACTIVE_STALE check (heartbeat timeout) already
233	        # catches truly dead workers.
234

248

295	def _determine_lifecycle(
296	    db_status: str,
297	    has_heartbeat: bool,
298	    heartbeat_age_sec: Optional[float],
299	    vram_total: Optional[int],
300	    vram_reported: bool,
301	    ready_for_tasks: bool,
302	    script_launched: bool,
303	    config: 'OrchestratorConfig',
304	    startup_phase: Optional[str] = None,
305	) -> WorkerLifecycle:
306	    """Map signals to lifecycle state."""
307	
308	    if db_status == 'terminated':
309	        return WorkerLifecycle.TERMINATED
310	
311	    if db_status == 'error':
312	        return WorkerLifecycle.ERROR
313	
314	    # If a heartbeat exists, the process has run at least once; treat it as active and
315	    # use heartbeat_age_sec only to decide "stale" vs "recent enough for promotion".
316	    if has_heartbeat:
317	        # Check for heartbeat staleness first
318	        if heartbeat_age_sec is not None and heartbeat_age_sec > config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec:
319	            return WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_STALE
320	
321	        # Not stale => active. Use startup_phase + VRAM to determine sub-state.
322	        # Workers signal progress via metadata.startup_phase:
323	        #   deps_installing → deps_verified → worker_starting → (worker enters poll loop)
324	        # Until the startup script finishes, treat as INITIALIZING even if
325	        # VRAM is reported (guardian heartbeats start before worker is ready).
326	        if startup_phase in ('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting'):
327	            return WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_INITIALIZING
328	        if vram_reported:
329	            if vram_total == 0:
330	                return WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_GPU_NOT_DETECTED
331	            else:
332	                return WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_READY
333	        return WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_INITIALIZING
334	
335	    # No heartbeat - still spawning
336	    if db_status in ('spawning', 'inactive', 'active'):
337	        # Note: 'active' without heartbeat can happen if manually promoted or legacy data
338	        if script_launched:
339	            return WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_RUNNING
340	        else:
341	            return WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_PENDING
342	
343	    # Fallback
344	    return WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_POD_PENDING
345	
346	
347	def _parse_timestamp(ts: Optional[str]) -> Optional[datetime]:
348	    """Parse ISO timestamp string to datetime."""
349	    if not ts:
350	        return None
351	    try:
352	        dt = datetime.fromisoformat(str(ts).replace('Z', '+00:00'))
353	        if dt.tzinfo is None:
354	            dt = dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
355	        return dt
356	    except Exception:
357	        return None
358	
359
```

40	@dataclass
41	class DerivedWorkerState:
42	    """
43	    Complete derived state for a worker at a point in time.
44	    All timeout checks and health assessments happen during derivation.
45	    """
46	
47	    # Identity
48	    worker_id: str
49	    runpod_id: Optional[str]
50	    lifecycle: WorkerLifecycle
51	    db_status: str  # Original DB status for reference
52	
53	    # Timing (all in seconds)
54	    created_at: datetime
55	    script_launched_at: Optional[datetime]
56	    effective_age_sec: float          # Time since script launch (or creation if no script)
57	    heartbeat_age_sec: Optional[float]  # Time since last heartbeat (None if never)
58	
59	    # Health signals
60	    has_heartbeat: bool
61	    heartbeat_is_recent: bool         # Within promotion threshold
62	    vram_total_mb: Optional[int]
63	    vram_used_mb: Optional[int]
64	    vram_reported: bool               # Has VRAM data been reported?
65	    ready_for_tasks: bool             # Worker explicitly signaled readiness
66	    in_startup_phase: bool
67	    has_ever_claimed_task: bool
68	    has_active_task: bool
69	
70	    # Derived health flags
71	    is_gpu_broken: bool               # VRAM=0 for too long
72	    is_not_claiming: bool             # Ready but not claiming for too long
73	    is_stale: bool                    # Heartbeat too old
74	
75	    # Action flags
76	    should_promote_to_active: bool    # Spawning worker with heartbeat
77	    should_terminate: bool            # Any termination condition met
78	    termination_reason: Optional[str]
79	    error_code: Optional[str]         # Structured error code for analytics
80	
81	    @property
82	    def is_healthy(self) -> bool:
83	        """Worker is in a healthy, productive state."""
84	        return self.lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_READY and not self.should_terminate
85	
86	    @property
87	    def is_spawning(self) -> bool:
88	        """Worker is still in spawning phase."""
89	        return self.lifecycle in (
90	            WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_POD_PENDING,
91	            WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_PENDING,
92	            WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_RUNNING,
93	        )
94	
95	    @property
96	    def is_active(self) -> bool:
97	        """Worker is in an active phase (has heartbeat)."""
98	        return self.lifecycle in (
99	            WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_INITIALIZING,
100	            WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_GPU_NOT_DETECTED,
101	            WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_READY,
102	            WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_STALE,
103	        )
104	
105	    @property
106	    def is_terminal(self) -> bool:
107	        """Worker is in a terminal state."""
108	        return self.lifecycle in (WorkerLifecycle.ERROR, WorkerLifecycle.TERMINATED)
109

111	def derive_worker_state(
112	    worker: Dict[str, Any],
113	    config: 'OrchestratorConfig',
114	    now: datetime,
115	    has_ever_claimed: bool,
116	    has_active_task: bool,
117	    queued_count: int
118	) -> DerivedWorkerState:
119	    """
120	    Single source of truth for worker state derivation.
121	
122	    All timeout checks happen here. The rest of the control loop
123	    just pattern-matches on the derived state.
124	
125	    Args:
126	        worker: Raw worker dict from database
127	        config: Orchestrator configuration
128	        now: Current timestamp (passed in for testability)
129	        has_ever_claimed: Whether worker has ever claimed any task
130	        has_active_task: Whether worker currently has an active task
131	        queued_count: Number of queued tasks (for "not claiming" checks)
132	
133	    Returns:
134	        DerivedWorkerState with all health assessments completed
135	    """
136	    worker_id = worker['id']
137	    metadata = worker.get('metadata', {}) or {}
138	    db_status = worker['status']
139	    runpod_id = metadata.get('runpod_id')
140	    startup_phase = metadata.get('startup_phase')
141	
142	    # Parse timestamps
143	    created_at = _parse_timestamp(worker.get('created_at')) or now
144	    script_launched_at = _parse_timestamp(metadata.get('startup_script_launched_at'))
145	    last_heartbeat = _parse_timestamp(worker.get('last_heartbeat'))
146	
147	    # Calculate ages
148	    age_basis = script_launched_at or created_at
149	    effective_age_sec = (now - age_basis).total_seconds()
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/live_test_workers.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Distinguishing live-test workers from production workers.\n\nThe orchestrator treats live-test workers as opaque to *capacity control* —\nthey don't count toward production scaling decisions, and the production\nphases (spawn handling, health checks) skip them, because their lifecycle is\nowned by an out-of-band harness on its own schedule.\n\nThey are NOT opaque to *cleanup*. The failsafe stale check and zombie\nreaper operate on every worker the orchestrator can see. Crashes leak GPUs\nregardless of who originally owned the pod, so the orchestrator is the\nsingle authority on liveness; ownership tags only affect accounting.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any, Mapping\n\n\ndef is_excluded_from_capacity_control(worker: Mapping[str, Any]) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return True when a worker should be ignored by production capacity math.\n\n    These workers are still subject to failsafe and zombie reaping — see\n    ``partition_capacity_workers`` for how the control loop applies the\n    distinction.\n    \"\"\"\n    metadata = worker.get(\"metadata\")\n    if not isinstance(metadata, Mapping):\n        return False\n\n    return bool(\n        metadata.get(\"live_test_variant\")\n        or metadata.get(\"live_test_run_id\")\n        or metadata.get(\"live_test\") is True\n    )\n\n\ndef partition_capacity_workers(\n    workers: list[dict[str, Any]],\n) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], list[dict[str, Any]]]:\n    \"\"\"Split workers into ``(production, excluded)`` lists.\n\n    Production workers participate in scaling, spawn handling, and active\n    health checks. Excluded workers are visible only to cleanup paths\n    (failsafe stale check, zombie reaper).\n    \"\"\"\n    production: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    excluded: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for worker in workers:\n        (excluded if is_excluded_from_capacity_control(worker) else production).append(worker)\n    return production, excluded\n"
}

1	"""
2	Worker state model for the GPU Orchestrator.
3	
4	Provides a single source of truth for worker state derivation:
5	- WorkerLifecycle enum: All possible worker states
6	- DerivedWorkerState: Complete computed state for a worker at a point in time

{

  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py",
  "old_string": "    async def _run_periodic_checks(\n        self,\n        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],\n        summary: CycleSummary\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run checks that only happen every Nth cycle.\"\"\"\n        # Create a mutable dict to track periodic check results\n        # We use a single dict so mutations persist across method calls\n        periodic_summary = {\n            'actions': {\n                'tasks_reset': 0,\n                'workers_failed': 0,\n                'workers_terminated': 0,\n            },\n            'storage_health': {}\n        }\n\n        # Storage health check\n        active_workers = []\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_active:\n                worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(ws.worker_id)\n                if worker:\n                    active_workers.append(worker)\n\n        await self._check_storage_health(active_workers, periodic_summary)\n\n        # Failsafe stale worker check\n        await self._failsafe_stale_worker_check(worker_states, periodic_summary)\n\n        # Copy results back to the main summary\n        summary.tasks_reset += periodic_summary['actions'].get('tasks_reset', 0)\n        summary.workers_failed += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_failed', 0)\n        summary.workers_terminated += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_terminated', 0)",
  "new_string": "    async def _run_periodic_checks(\n        self,\n        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],\n        excluded_workers: List[Dict],\n        queued_count: int,\n        summary: CycleSummary,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run checks that only happen every Nth cycle.\"\"\"\n        # Create a mutable dict to track periodic check results\n        # We use a single dict so mutations persist across method calls\n        periodic_summary = {\n            'actions': {\n                'tasks_reset': 0,\n                'workers_failed': 0,\n                'workers_terminated': 0,\n            },\n            'storage_health': {}\n        }\n\n        # Storage health check (production workers only — harness owns its volumes)\n        active_workers = []\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_active:\n                worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(ws.worker_id)\n                if worker:\n                    active_workers.append(worker)\n\n        await self._check_storage_health(active_workers, periodic_summary)\n\n        # Failsafe stale worker check applies to ALL workers — production AND\n        # excluded. Cleanup is the orchestrator's job regardless of who owns\n        # the spawn lifecycle, so harness crashes don't leak GPUs.\n        excluded_states = await self._derive_all_worker_states(excluded_workers, queued_count) if excluded_workers else []\n        all_states = worker_states + excluded_states\n        await self._failsafe_stale_worker_check(all_states, periodic_summary)\n\n        # Copy results back to the main summary\n        summary.tasks_reset += periodic_summary['actions'].get('tasks_reset', 0)\n        summary.workers_failed += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_failed', 0)\n        summary.workers_terminated += periodic_summary['actions'].get('workers_terminated', 0)"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py",
  "old_string": "    async def _failsafe_stale_worker_check(self, worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState], summary: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Failsafe check for workers with very stale heartbeats.\"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        stale_workers: List[Tuple[DerivedWorkerState, float]] = []\n\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_terminal:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.in_startup_phase:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.should_terminate:\n                continue\n\n            # Idle healthy workers are PATH B's responsibility (scaling execution)\n            if ws.is_active and not ws.has_active_task and ws.heartbeat_is_recent:\n                continue\n\n            cutoff_sec = self.config.spawning_timeout_sec if ws.is_spawning else self.config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec\n            age = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec\n            if age is not None and age > cutoff_sec:\n                stale_workers.append((ws, age))",
  "new_string": "    async def _failsafe_stale_worker_check(self, worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState], summary: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Failsafe check for workers that should be reaped.\n\n        Catches three failure modes:\n        1. Wall-clock cap exceeded (max_lifetime_sec) — fires even with a\n           fresh heartbeat. Catches harness pods that poll forever after a\n           crash, where heartbeats stay current but no work ever runs.\n        2. Stuck in spawning past spawning_timeout_sec.\n        3. Stale heartbeat past gpu_idle_timeout_sec.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        stale_workers: List[Tuple[DerivedWorkerState, float]] = []\n\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_terminal:\n                continue\n\n            # (1) Hard wall-clock cap. Checked BEFORE the recent-heartbeat\n            # short-circuit so a healthy-looking but indefinitely-polling\n            # worker still gets reaped.\n            if ws.max_lifetime_sec is not None and ws.effective_age_sec > ws.max_lifetime_sec:\n                stale_workers.append((ws, ws.effective_age_sec))\n                continue\n\n            if ws.in_startup_phase:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.should_terminate:\n                continue\n\n            # Idle healthy workers are PATH B's responsibility (scaling execution).\n            # Excluded workers don't go through PATH B, so the wall-clock cap\n            # above is the only thing that bounds their lifetime — that's\n            # intentional.\n            if ws.is_active and not ws.has_active_task and ws.heartbeat_is_recent:\n                continue\n\n            cutoff_sec = self.config.spawning_timeout_sec if ws.is_spawning else self.config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec\n            age = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec\n            if age is not None and age > cutoff_sec:\n                stale_workers.append((ws, age))"
}

1	"""State acquisition and derivation phase for orchestrator cycles."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import asyncio
6	import logging
7	from datetime import datetime, timezone
8	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
9	
10	from gpu_orchestrator.live_test_workers import filter_live_test_workers
11	from gpu_orchestrator.worker_state import DerivedWorkerState, TaskCounts, derive_worker_state
12	
13	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
14	
15	if TYPE_CHECKING:
16	    from gpu_orchestrator.control.contracts import ControlHost
17	
18	
19	class StatePhaseMixin:
20	    """Phase methods for fetching current system state and deriving worker lifecycle."""
21	
22	    async def _fetch_current_state(
23	        self: "ControlHost",
24	    ) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], TaskCounts, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
25	        """Fetch workers and task counts from database. Returns (workers, task_counts, detailed_counts)."""
26	        # Only fetch non-terminated workers for the main control loop.
27	        # Terminated workers don't need lifecycle checks, and including them
28	        # bloats the .in_() queries in batch_check_ever_claimed / batch_check_active_tasks
29	        # past PostgREST's URL length limit, causing 400 errors.
30	        fetched_workers = await self.db.get_workers(status=["spawning", "active", "error"])
31	        workers = filter_live_test_workers(fetched_workers)
32	        ignored_live_workers = len(fetched_workers) - len(workers)
33	        if ignored_live_workers:
34	            logger.info(
35	                "Ignoring %s live-test worker(s) for production capacity control",
36	                ignored_live_workers,
37	            )
38	
39	        detailed_counts = await self.db.get_detailed_task_counts_via_edge_function()
40	
41	        if detailed_counts:
42	            aggregate_totals = detailed_counts.get("totals", {})
43	            totals = detailed_counts.get("selected_pool_totals") or aggregate_totals
44	            active_cloud_count = totals.get("active_only", 0)
45	
46	            potentially_claimable = totals.get("potentially_claimable")
47	
48	            if potentially_claimable is not None:
49	                queued_count = potentially_claimable
50	                total_tasks = potentially_claimable + active_cloud_count
51	
52	                queued_only = totals.get("queued_only", 0)
53	                blocked_capacity = totals.get("blocked_by_capacity", 0)
54	                blocked_deps = totals.get("blocked_by_deps", 0)
55	                blocked_settings = totals.get("blocked_by_settings", 0)
56	                route_keys = totals.get("route_keys")
57	
58	                logger.debug(
59	                    f"TASK COUNT (Cycle #{self.cycle_count}): "
60	                    f"Scaling={potentially_claimable} "
61	                    f"(queued_only={queued_only} + capacity_blocked={blocked_capacity}), "
62	                    f"Active={active_cloud_count}, "
63	                    f"[NOT scaling for: deps_blocked={blocked_deps}, settings_blocked={blocked_settings}], "
64	                    f"route_keys={route_keys}"
65	                )
66	            else:
67	                queued_count = totals.get("queued_only", 0)
68	                total_tasks = totals.get("queued_plus_active", 0)
69	                logger.debug(
70	                    f"TASK COUNT (Cycle #{self.cycle_count}): "
71	                    f"Queued={queued_count}, Active={active_cloud_count}, Total={total_tasks}"
72	                )
73	
74	            self._log_detailed_task_breakdown(detailed_counts, queued_count, active_cloud_count, total_tasks)
75	        else:
76	            logger.warning("Failed to get detailed task counts, falling back to simple counting")
77	            total_tasks = await self.db.count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(include_active=True)
78	            queued_count = await self.db.count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(include_active=False)
79	            active_cloud_count = total_tasks - queued_count
80	            logger.warning(f"Fallback returned: {total_tasks} total, {queued_count} queued")
81	
82	        logger.info(f"Cycle #{self.cycle_count}: scaling={queued_count} active={active_cloud_count}")
83	        return workers, TaskCounts(queued=queued_count, active_cloud=active_cloud_count, total=total_tasks), detailed_counts
84	
85	    async def _derive_all_worker_states(
86	        self: "ControlHost",
87	        workers: List[Dict[str, Any]],
88	        queued_count: int,
89	    ) -> List[DerivedWorkerState]:
90	        """Derive state for all workers using batch queries."""
91	        worker_ids = [w["id"] for w in workers]
92	
93	        claimed_map, active_tasks_map = await asyncio.gather(
94	            self.db.batch_check_ever_claimed(worker_ids),
95	            self.db.batch_check_active_tasks(worker_ids),
96	        )
97	
98	        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
99	        return [
100	            derive_worker_state(
101	                worker=w,
102	                config=self.config,
103	                now=now,
104	                has_ever_claimed=claimed_map.get(w["id"], False),
105	                has_active_task=active_tasks_map.get(w["id"], False),
106	                queued_count=queued_count,
107	            )
108	            for w in workers
109	        ]
110	
111	
112	__all__ = ["StatePhaseMixin"]
113

1	"""Periodic maintenance checks (storage, stale workers, zombie reconciliation)."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import logging
6	import os
7	from datetime import datetime, timezone
8	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Dict, List
9	
10	import runpod
11	from gpu_orchestrator.control.diagnostics import WorkerDiagnosticsMixin
12	from gpu_orchestrator.live_test_workers import is_live_test_worker
13	from gpu_orchestrator.worker_state import CycleSummary, DerivedWorkerState
14	
15	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
16	
17	if TYPE_CHECKING:
18	    from gpu_orchestrator.control.contracts import ControlHost
19	
20	
21	class PeriodicChecksMixin:
22	    """Periodic check methods executed every cycle or every N cycles."""
23	
24	    async def _run_periodic_checks(
25	        self: "ControlHost",
26	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
27	        summary: CycleSummary,
28	    ) -> None:
29	        """Run checks that only happen every Nth cycle."""
30	        if not isinstance(self, WorkerDiagnosticsMixin):
31	            logger.debug("Periodic checks host is missing diagnostics mixin methods")
32	
33	        periodic_summary: Dict[str, Any] = {
34	            "actions": {
35	                "tasks_reset": 0,
36	                "workers_failed": 0,
37	                "workers_terminated": 0,
38	            },
39	            "storage_health": {},
40	        }
41	
42	        active_workers = []
43	        for ws in worker_states:
44	            if ws.is_active:
45	                worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(ws.worker_id)
46	                if worker:
47	                    active_workers.append(worker)
48	
49	        await self._check_storage_health(active_workers, periodic_summary)
50	
51	        await self._failsafe_stale_worker_check(worker_states, periodic_summary)
52	
53	        summary.tasks_reset += periodic_summary["actions"].get("tasks_reset", 0)
54	        summary.workers_failed += periodic_summary["actions"].get("workers_failed", 0)
55	        summary.workers_terminated += periodic_summary["actions"].get("workers_terminated", 0)
56	
57	    async def _check_storage_health(
58	        self: "ControlHost",
59	        active_workers: List[Dict[str, Any]],
60	        summary: Dict[str, Any],
61	    ) -> None:
62	        """Check storage health across all storage volumes."""
63	        if self.cycle_count - self.last_storage_check_cycle < self.config.storage_check_interval_cycles:
64	            return
65	
66	        self.last_storage_check_cycle = self.cycle_count
67	        logger.info(f"STORAGE_CHECK Starting periodic check (cycle {self.cycle_count})")
68	
69	        workers_by_storage: Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]] = {}
70	        for worker in active_workers:
71	            storage_volume = worker.get("metadata", {}).get("storage_volume")
72	            if storage_volume:
73	                if storage_volume not in workers_by_storage:
74	                    workers_by_storage[storage_volume] = []
75	                workers_by_storage[storage_volume].append(worker)
76	
77	        if not workers_by_storage:
78	            logger.info("STORAGE_CHECK No active workers with storage volume info")
79	            return
80	
81	        if "storage_health" not in summary:
82	            summary["storage_health"] = {}
83	
84	        for storage_name, storage_workers in workers_by_storage.items():
85	            try:
86	                volume_id = self.runpod._get_storage_volume_id(storage_name)
87	                if not volume_id:
88	                    continue
89	
90	                check_worker = None
91	                for worker in storage_workers:
92	                    if worker.get("last_heartbeat"):
93	                        try:
94	                            heartbeat_time = datetime.fromisoformat(worker["last_heartbeat"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
95	                            heartbeat_age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - heartbeat_time).total_seconds()
96	                            if heartbeat_age < 60:
97	                                runpod_id = worker.get("metadata", {}).get("runpod_id")
98	                                if runpod_id:
99	                                    check_worker = worker
100	                                    break
101	                        except Exception as exc:
102	                            logger.debug(
103	                                "STORAGE_CHECK skipping worker heartbeat parse for %s: %s",
104	                                worker.get("id"),
105	                                exc,
106	                            )
107	                            continue
108	
109	                if not check_worker:
110	                    continue
111	
112	                runpod_id = check_worker.get("metadata", {}).get("runpod_id")
113	                health = self.runpod.check_storage_health(
114	                    storage_name=storage_name,
115	                    volume_id=volume_id,
116	                    active_runpod_id=runpod_id,
117	                    min_free_gb=self.config.storage_min_free_gb,
118	                    max_percent_used=self.config.storage_max_percent_used,
119	                )
120	
121	                summary["storage_health"][storage_name] = health
122	
123	                if health.get("needs_expansion"):
124	                    current_size = health.get("total_gb", 100)
125	                    new_size = current_size + self.config.storage_expansion_increment_gb
126	
127	                    logger.warning(f"STORAGE_CHECK '{storage_name}' needs expansion: {health.get('message')}")
128	                    if self.runpod._expand_network_volume(volume_id, new_size):
129	                        logger.info(f"STORAGE_CHECK Successfully expanded '{storage_name}' to {new_size}GB")
130	                    else:
131	                        logger.error(f"STORAGE_CHECK Failed to expand '{storage_name}'!")
132	
133	            except Exception as exc:
134	                logger.error(f"STORAGE_CHECK Error checking '{storage_name}': {exc}")
135	
136	    async def _check_error_worker_cleanup(self: "ControlHost", worker: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
137	        """Check workers marked as error and ensure RunPod instances are terminated."""
138	        worker_id = worker["id"]
139	        metadata = worker.get("metadata", {})
140	        runpod_id = metadata.get("runpod_id")
141	        error_time_str = metadata.get("error_time")
142	
143	        if error_time_str:
144	            try:
145	                error_time = datetime.fromisoformat(error_time_str.replace("Z", "+00:00"))
146	                error_age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - error_time).total_seconds()
147	
148	                if error_age > self.config.error_cleanup_grace_period_sec:
149	                    logger.info(
150	                        f"[ERROR_CLEANUP] Worker {worker_id} past grace period ({error_age:.0f}s), forcing cleanup"
151	                    )
152	
153	                    if runpod_id:
154	                        pod_status = self.runpod.get_pod_status(runpod_id)
155	                        if pod_status and pod_status.get("desired_status") not in ["TERMINATED", "FAILED"]:
156	                            try:
157	                                self.runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)
158	                            except Exception as exc:
159	                                logger.error(f"Failed to force terminate RunPod {runpod_id}: {exc}")
160	
161	                    termination_metadata = {"terminated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()}
162	                    await self.db.update_worker_status(worker_id, "terminated", termination_metadata)
163	
164	            except Exception as exc:
165	                logger.error(f"Error parsing error_time for worker {worker_id}: {exc}")
166	        else:
167	            logger.warning(f"[ERROR_CLEANUP] Worker {worker_id} has no error_time, forcing immediate cleanup")
168	            if runpod_id:
169	                try:
170	                    self.runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)
171	                    termination_metadata = {"terminated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()}
172	                    await self.db.update_worker_status(worker_id, "terminated", termination_metadata)
173	                except Exception as exc:
174	                    logger.error(f"[ERROR_CLEANUP] Failed to cleanup legacy error worker {worker_id}: {exc}")
175	
176	    async def _failsafe_stale_worker_check(
177	        self: "ControlHost",
178	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
179	        summary: Dict[str, Any],
180	    ) -> None:
181	        """Failsafe check for workers with very stale heartbeats."""
182	        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
183	        stale_workers: List[DerivedWorkerState] = []
184	
185	        for ws in worker_states:
186	            if ws.is_terminal:
187	                continue
188	
189	            if ws.in_startup_phase:
190	                continue
191	
192	            if ws.should_terminate:
193	                continue
194	
195	            # Idle healthy workers are PATH B's responsibility (scaling execution)
196	            if ws.is_active and not ws.has_active_task and ws.heartbeat_is_recent:
197	                continue
198	
199	            cutoff_sec = self.config.spawning_timeout_sec if ws.is_spawning else self.config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec
200	            age = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec
201	            if age is not None and age > cutoff_sec:
202	                stale_workers.append(ws)
203	
204	        for ws in stale_workers:
205	            worker_id = ws.worker_id
206	            age_sec = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec
207	            logger.warning(f"FAILSAFE: Worker {worker_id} has stale heartbeat ({age_sec:.0f}s)")
208	
209	            try:
210	                worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(worker_id)
211	                if not worker:
212	                    continue
213	
214	                runpod_id = ws.runpod_id
215	                if runpod_id:
216	                    pod_status = self.runpod.get_pod_status(runpod_id)
217	                    if pod_status and pod_status.get("desired_status") not in ["TERMINATED", "FAILED"]:
218	                        self.runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)
219	
220	                reset_count = await self.db.reset_orphaned_tasks([worker_id])
221	                if reset_count > 0:
222	                    summary["actions"]["tasks_reset"] = summary["actions"].get("tasks_reset", 0) + reset_count
223	
224	                metadata_update = {
225	                    "error_reason": f"FAILSAFE: Stale heartbeat with status {ws.db_status}",
226	                    "error_time": now.isoformat(),
227	                }
228	                await self.db.update_worker_status(worker_id, "terminated", metadata_update)
229	                summary["actions"]["workers_failed"] = summary["actions"].get("workers_failed", 0) + 1
230	
231	            except Exception as exc:
232	                logger.error(f"FAILSAFE: Failed to cleanup stale worker {worker_id}: {exc}")
233	
234	        await self._efficient_zombie_check(summary)
235	
236	    async def _efficient_zombie_check(self: "ControlHost", summary: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
237	        """Bidirectional zombie detection."""
238	        if self.cycle_count % 10 != 0:
239	            return
240	
241	        try:
242	            logger.info("Running efficient zombie check (every 10th cycle)")
243	
244	            runpod.api_key = os.getenv("RUNPOD_API_KEY")
245	
246	            pods = runpod.get_pods()
247	            gpu_worker_pods = [
248	                pod
249	                for pod in pods
250	                if pod.get("name", "").startswith("gpu-")
251	                and pod.get("desiredStatus") not in ["TERMINATED", "FAILED"]
252	            ]
253	
254	            if not gpu_worker_pods:
255	                return
256	
257	            db_workers = await self.db.get_workers()
258	            db_worker_names = {w["id"] for w in db_workers if w["status"] != "terminated"}
259	
260	            for pod in gpu_worker_pods:
261	                pod_name = pod.get("name")
262	                runpod_id = pod.get("id")
263	
264	                if pod_name not in db_worker_names:
265	                    logger.warning(f"ZOMBIE DETECTED: RunPod pod {pod_name} ({runpod_id}) not in database")
266	                    try:
267	                        success = self.runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)
268	                        if success:
269	                            summary["actions"]["workers_terminated"] = (
270	                                summary["actions"].get("workers_terminated", 0) + 1
271	                            )
272	                    except Exception as exc:
273	                        logger.error(f"ZOMBIE CLEANUP: Error terminating {runpod_id}: {exc}")
274	
275	            runpod_pod_ids = {pod.get("id") for pod in gpu_worker_pods}
276	            runpod_pod_names = {pod.get("name") for pod in gpu_worker_pods}
277	
278	            for worker in db_workers:
279	                if is_live_test_worker(worker):
280	                    continue
281	                if worker["status"] in ["active", "spawning"]:
282	                    runpod_id = worker.get("metadata", {}).get("runpod_id")
283	                    worker_id = worker["id"]
284	
285	                    if runpod_id and runpod_id not in runpod_pod_ids and worker_id not in runpod_pod_names:
286	                        logger.warning(
287	                            f"EXTERNALLY TERMINATED: Worker {worker_id} not found in RunPod"
288	                        )
289	                        try:
290	                            await self._mark_worker_error(
291	                                worker,
292	                                "Pod externally terminated - not found in RunPod",
293	                            )
294	                            summary["actions"]["workers_failed"] = summary["actions"].get("workers_failed", 0) + 1
295	                        except Exception as exc:
296	                            logger.error(
297	                                f"Error marking externally terminated worker {worker_id}: {exc}"
298	                            )
299	
300	        except Exception as exc:
301	            logger.error(f"Error during efficient zombie check: {exc}")
302	
303	
304	__all__ = ["PeriodicChecksMixin"]

Returns (workers, task_counts, detailed_counts).\"\"\"\n        # Only fetch non-terminated workers for the main control loop.\n        # Terminated workers don't need lifecycle checks, and including them\n        # bloats the .in_() queries in batch_check_ever_claimed / batch_check_active_tasks\n        # past PostgREST's URL length limit, causing 400 errors.\n        fetched_workers = await self.db.get_workers(status=[\"spawning\", \"active\", \"error\"])\n        workers = filter_live_test_workers(fetched_workers)\n        ignored_live_workers = len(fetched_workers) - len(workers)\n        if ignored_live_workers:\n            logger.info(\n                \"Ignoring %s live-test worker(s) for production capacity control\",\n                ignored_live_workers,\n            )",

}

  "new_string": "    async def _run_periodic_checks(\n        self: \"ControlHost\",\n        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],\n        excluded_workers: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n        queued_count: int,\n        summary: CycleSummary,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run checks that only happen every Nth cycle.\"\"\"\n        if not isinstance(self, WorkerDiagnosticsMixin):\n            logger.debug(\"Periodic checks host is missing diagnostics mixin methods\")\n\n        periodic_summary: Dict[str, Any] = {\n            \"actions\": {\n                \"tasks_reset\": 0,\n                \"workers_failed\": 0,\n                \"workers_terminated\": 0,\n            },\n            \"storage_health\": {},\n        }\n\n        active_workers = []\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_active:\n                worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(ws.worker_id)\n                if worker:\n                    active_workers.append(worker)\n\n        await self._check_storage_health(active_workers, periodic_summary)\n\n        # Failsafe applies to ALL workers — including excluded ones — so\n        # harness crashes can't leak GPUs.\n        excluded_states = await self._derive_all_worker_states(excluded_workers, queued_count) if excluded_workers else []\n        await self._failsafe_stale_worker_check(worker_states + excluded_states, periodic_summary)"

  "old_string": "    async def _failsafe_stale_worker_check(\n        self: \"ControlHost\",\n        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],\n        summary: Dict[str, Any],\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Failsafe check for workers with very stale heartbeats.\"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        stale_workers: List[DerivedWorkerState] = []\n\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_terminal:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.in_startup_phase:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.should_terminate:\n                continue\n\n            # Idle healthy workers are PATH B's responsibility (scaling execution)\n            if ws.is_active and not ws.has_active_task and ws.heartbeat_is_recent:\n                continue\n\n            cutoff_sec = self.config.spawning_timeout_sec if ws.is_spawning else self.config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec\n            age = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec\n            if age is not None and age > cutoff_sec:\n                stale_workers.append(ws)",
  "new_string": "    async def _failsafe_stale_worker_check(\n        self: \"ControlHost\",\n        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],\n        summary: Dict[str, Any],\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Failsafe check for workers that should be reaped.\n\n        Catches three failure modes: wall-clock cap exceeded, stuck spawning,\n        stale heartbeat. The wall-clock cap fires even with a fresh heartbeat\n        so harness pods that poll forever after a crash still get reaped.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        stale_workers: List[DerivedWorkerState] = []\n\n        for ws in worker_states:\n            if ws.is_terminal:\n                continue\n\n            # Hard wall-clock cap — checked BEFORE the recent-heartbeat\n            # short-circuit so a healthy-looking but indefinitely-polling\n            # worker still gets reaped.\n            if ws.max_lifetime_sec is not None and ws.effective_age_sec > ws.max_lifetime_sec:\n                stale_workers.append(ws)\n                continue\n\n            if ws.in_startup_phase:\n                continue\n\n            if ws.should_terminate:\n                continue\n\n            # Idle healthy workers are PATH B's responsibility (scaling execution).\n            # Excluded workers don't go through PATH B, so the cap above is\n            # what bounds their lifetime.\n            if ws.is_active and not ws.has_active_task and ws.heartbeat_is_recent:\n                continue\n\n            cutoff_sec = self.config.spawning_timeout_sec if ws.is_spawning else self.config.gpu_idle_timeout_sec\n            age = ws.heartbeat_age_sec if ws.has_heartbeat else ws.effective_age_sec\n            if age is not None and age > cutoff_sec:\n                stale_workers.append(ws)"
}

71	        "startup_script_launched": True,
72	        "startup_script_launched_at": "2026-03-30T00:00:10+00:00",

82	        "last_heartbeat": last_heartbeat,
83	        "metadata": metadata,
84	    }
85

89	        worker=_make_worker(startup_phase=startup_phase, last_heartbeat=last_heartbeat),
90	        config=make_config(),

136	    """SPAWNING_SCRIPT_RUNNING gets script_running_timeout_sec (1800s), not spawning_timeout_sec (600s)."""
137	    # At 15 minutes (900s) — past spawning_timeout (600s) but within script_running (1800s)

155	    )
156	
157	    assert derived.lifecycle == WorkerLifecycle.SPAWNING_SCRIPT_RUNNING
158	    assert derived.should_terminate is True
159	    assert derived.error_code == "SCRIPT_RUNNING_TIMEOUT"
160	
161	
162	def test_heartbeating_worker_with_stale_ready_phase_not_killed():
163	    """A heartbeating worker with startup_phase='ready' must NOT be killed by startup timeout."""
164	    # Worker has been alive 40 minutes, heartbeating, startup_phase still "ready"

169	    )

173	    assert derived.should_terminate is False

11	def make_config(**overrides) -> OrchestratorConfig:
12	    """Create a config for testing."""
13	    config_values = {
14	        "min_active_gpus": 0,

  "old_string": "    asyncio.run(host._failsafe_stale_worker_check([startup_state], summary))\n\n    host.db.get_worker_by_id.assert_not_awaited()\n    host.db.reset_orphaned_tasks.assert_not_awaited()\n    host.runpod.get_pod_status.assert_not_called()\n    host.runpod.terminate_worker.assert_not_called()\n    assert summary[\"actions\"] == {\"tasks_reset\": 0, \"workers_failed\": 0, \"workers_terminated\": 0}",
  "new_string": "    asyncio.run(host._failsafe_stale_worker_check([startup_state], summary))\n\n    host.db.get_worker_by_id.assert_not_awaited()\n    host.db.reset_orphaned_tasks.assert_not_awaited()\n    host.runpod.get_pod_status.assert_not_called()\n    host.runpod.terminate_worker.assert_not_called()\n    assert summary[\"actions\"] == {\"tasks_reset\": 0, \"workers_failed\": 0, \"workers_terminated\": 0}\n\n\ndef test_failsafe_reaps_excluded_worker_past_max_lifetime():\n    \"\"\"A live-test worker with a fresh heartbeat but past its wall-clock cap\n    must be reaped. This is the regression test for the leaked-pod incident:\n    harness pods polled forever after a crash because heartbeats stayed\n    current and nothing else bounded their lifetime.\n    \"\"\"\n    excluded_state = replace(\n        make_worker_state(\n            worker_id=\"worker-livetest\",\n            lifecycle=WorkerLifecycle.ACTIVE_READY,\n            excluded_from_capacity_control=True,\n            max_lifetime_sec=7200,\n        ),\n        effective_age_sec=10_000,  # past the 7200s cap\n        heartbeat_age_sec=5,        # heartbeat is fresh\n        heartbeat_is_recent=True,\n        has_heartbeat=True,\n    )\n\n    captured_worker = {\n        \"id\": \"worker-livetest\",\n        \"status\": \"active\",\n        \"metadata\": {\"runpod_id\": \"runpod-worker-livetest\", \"live_test_variant\": \"fresh\"},\n    }\n\n    class Host(periodic.PeriodicChecksMixin):\n        def __init__(self):\n            self.config = make_config()\n            self.db = SimpleNamespace(\n                get_worker_by_id=AsyncMock(return_value=captured_worker),\n                reset_orphaned_tasks=AsyncMock(return_value=0),\n                update_worker_status=AsyncMock(return_value=True),\n                get_workers=AsyncMock(return_value=[]),\n            )\n            runpod = Mock()\n            runpod.get_pod_status = Mock(return_value={\"desired_status\": \"RUNNING\"})\n            runpod.terminate_worker = Mock(return_value=True)\n            self.runpod = runpod\n            self.cycle_count = 1\n            self.last_storage_check_cycle = 0\n\n    host = Host()\n    summary = {\"actions\": {\"tasks_reset\": 0, \"workers_failed\": 0, \"workers_terminated\": 0}}\n\n    asyncio.run(host._failsafe_stale_worker_check([excluded_state], summary))\n\n    host.runpod.terminate_worker.assert_called_once_with(\"runpod-worker-livetest\")\n    host.db.update_worker_status.assert_awaited_once()\n    args, _ = host.db.update_worker_status.await_args\n    assert args[0] == \"worker-livetest\"\n    assert args[1] == \"terminated\"\n    assert summary[\"actions\"][\"workers_failed\"] == 1"

```json
{
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nMake orchestrator the sole authority on worker liveness\n\nLive-test workers were excluded from production capacity control AND from\ncleanup, conflating accounting with lifecycle ownership. When a live-test\nharness run crashed (commonly: pod ran out of disk mid-matrix), its pods\nleaked indefinitely because the orchestrator's failsafe and zombie reapers\nnever saw them. Four GPUs were burning when this surfaced.\n\nSplit the two concerns:\n- Capacity accounting still excludes live-test workers (no behaviour change).\n- Cleanup paths now see every worker. Failsafe and zombie reaper run on the\n  full set, so harness crashes can't leak GPUs by construction.\n\nAlso adds a wall-clock cap (`max_lifetime_sec`) read by the failsafe before\nthe recent-heartbeat short-circuit, so harness pods that poll forever after\na crash with fresh heartbeats still get reaped. Defaults to 7200s for\nexcluded workers, unbounded for production. Override per-pod via\n`metadata.max_lifetime_sec` or fleet-wide via `EXCLUDED_WORKER_MAX_LIFETIME_SEC`.\n\nRenamed `is_live_test_worker` → `is_excluded_from_capacity_control` so the\nname encodes the legitimate use; the old name read correctly at the\nzombie-reaper skip too, which is exactly how the bug crept in.\n\nMirrored the fix in the in-progress `control/phases/` mixin modules so the\nbug doesn't recur when they're activated.\n\nRegression test: a worker tagged `live_test_variant` with a fresh heartbeat\nbut past its `max_lifetime_sec` is reaped by the failsafe.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit the fix"
}

{
  "command": "gh pr create --base megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts --title \"Make orchestrator the sole authority on worker liveness\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n- Live-test workers were excluded from production capacity control AND from cleanup, conflating accounting with lifecycle ownership. When a harness run crashed (commonly: pod ran out of disk mid-matrix), pods leaked indefinitely because the failsafe and zombie reaper never saw them. Four GPUs were burning when this surfaced.\n- Splits the two concerns: capacity accounting still excludes live-test (no behaviour change); cleanup paths now see every worker.\n- Adds a wall-clock cap (\\`max_lifetime_sec\\`, default 7200s for excluded workers, unbounded for production) checked **before** the recent-heartbeat short-circuit — so harness pods that poll forever after a crash with fresh heartbeats also get reaped.\n- Renamed \\`is_live_test_worker\\` → \\`is_excluded_from_capacity_control\\` so the name encodes the legitimate use; the old name read correctly at the zombie-reaper skip too, which is how the bug crept in.\n- Mirrored the fix in the in-progress \\`control/phases/\\` mixin modules so the bug doesn't recur when they're activated.\n\n## Invariant\nThe orchestrator is the sole authority on pod liveness. Ownership tags only affect capacity accounting, never cleanup.\n\n## Repro / how the leak happened\n1. Live-test matrix harness spawns a \\`gpu-*\\` pod via \\`worker_spawner\\` and tags its DB row with \\`live_test_run_id\\` / \\`live_test_variant\\`.\n2. Pod fills its 50GB disk (WAN 14B alone exceeds it), worker process crashes.\n3. Either heartbeat goes stale OR the polling loop survives and keeps heartbeating with no work.\n4. The orchestrator's \\`_fetch_current_state\\` filtered the row out before *any* downstream phase saw it — so failsafe didn't run, and the \\`_efficient_zombie_check\\` also explicitly skipped live-test rows.\n5. Pod runs forever. Repeat per failed run → 4 leaked pods over two days.\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] New regression test: \\`test_failsafe_reaps_excluded_worker_past_max_lifetime\\` covers the \"fresh heartbeat, past wall-clock cap\" case (the most insidious of the four leaked pods).\n- [x] Existing rename / partition test updated.\n- [x] Full suite: 158 passed.\n- [ ] After deploy: watch \\`Cycle # …\\` logs on Railway for one or two periodic-check cycles; confirm new log line \\`\"Ignoring N worker(s) for production capacity control (cleanup still applies)\"\\` and verify failsafe doesn't false-positive on the currently-active live-test pod (\\`reigh-live-test-fresh-20260509t171451z\\`).\n\n## Config\nNew env var: \\`EXCLUDED_WORKER_MAX_LIFETIME_SEC\\` (default \\`7200\\`). Per-pod override via \\`metadata.max_lifetime_sec\\`.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create PR"
}
